    }

    #[test]
    fn device_report_section_order_lists_battery_last_known_section() {
        let order = device_report_section_order();
        let bat = order
            .iter()
            .position(|s| *s == "Battery (com.apple.mobile.battery)")
            .unwrap();
        let other = order.iter().position(|s| *s == "Other").unwrap();
        assert!(bat < other, "Battery should render before Other");
    }

    #[test]
    fn diagnostic_get_values_table_is_unique_and_well_formed() {
        // No (label, domain, key) triple repeats — collision would silently lose a row.
        let mut seen = std::collections::HashSet::new();
        for (label, domain, key) in LOCKDOWN_DIAGNOSTIC_GET_VALUES {
            assert!(!label.is_empty(), "diagnostic row has empty label");
            assert!(seen.insert((*label, *domain, *key)), "duplicate row {:?}", label);
        }
        // Common identity keys we rely on must be present.
        let labels: Vec<&str> = LOCKDOWN_DIAGNOSTIC_GET_VALUES
            .iter()
            .map(|(l, _, _)| *l)
            .collect();
        for needed in ["DeviceName", "ProductVersion", "WiFiAddress"] {
            assert!(labels.contains(&needed), "missing {} in diagnostic table", needed);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn report_groups_and_annotates_storage() {
        let identity_xml = r#"<plist><dict><key>Value</key><string>iPhone15,3</string></dict></plist>"#;
        let storage_xml = r#"<plist><dict><key>Value</key><integer>256000000000</integer></dict></plist>"#;
        let battery_xml = r#"<plist><dict><key>Value</key><dict>
<key>BatteryCurrentCapacity</key><integer>87</integer>
<key>FullyCharged</key><false/>
</dict></dict></plist>"#;
        let prohibited = r#"<plist><dict><key>Error</key><string>GetProhibited</string></dict></plist>"#;

        let rows: Vec<(&str, &str, &str, Result<String, String>)> = vec![
            ("ProductType", "", "ProductType", Ok(identity_xml.into())),
            ("PhoneNumber", "", "PhoneNumber", Ok(prohibited.into())),
            (
                "TotalDiskCapacity",
                "com.apple.disk_usage",
                "TotalDiskCapacity",
                Ok(storage_xml.into()),
            ),
            (
                "Mobile.battery",
                "com.apple.mobile.battery",
                "",
                Ok(battery_xml.into()),
            ),
        ];

        let s = format_lockdown_device_report(&rows);

        // Sectioned output, in canonical order.
        let id_pos = s.find("Identity\n").expect("Identity section present");
        let net_pos = s
            .find("Network / cellular\n")
            .expect("Network section present");
        let storage_pos = s
            .find("Storage (disk_usage)\n")
            .expect("Storage section present");
        let bat_pos = s
            .find("Battery (com.apple.mobile.battery)\n")
            .expect("Battery section present");
        assert!(id_pos < net_pos);
        assert!(net_pos < storage_pos);
        assert!(storage_pos < bat_pos);

        // Identity values render as scalar key:value rows.
        assert!(s.contains("ProductType"));
        assert!(s.contains("iPhone15,3"));

        // GetProhibited shows up but doesn't break formatting.
        assert!(s.contains("Error: GetProhibited"));

        // Storage byte counts get a human-readable suffix.
        assert!(s.contains("256000000000"));
        assert!(s.contains("GiB"));

        // Battery dict expands as multi-line, indented sub-block.
        assert!(s.contains("BatteryCurrentCapacity: 87"));
        assert!(s.contains("FullyCharged: false"));
    }
}

===== ARCHIVO: vendor/idevice-usb-core/src/pcapd_proto.rs =====

//! `com.apple.pcapd` protocol helpers — pure parsing / framing, no I/O.
//!
//! Mirrors `pymobiledevice3/services/pcapd.py`. iOS exposes a **remote virtual interface (RVI)** that
//! mirrors network traffic to lockdownd. Each message on the (TLS-wrapped) service socket is a
//! standard `property_list_service` frame — `u32 BE length + binary plist` — and the plist payload
//! is a single **CFData** carrying the **`device_packet_struct`** below:
//!
//! ```text
//! struct device_packet {
//!     u32 BE  header_length;        // offset to packet bytes (so this header may grow)
//!     u8       header_version;
//!     u32 BE  packet_length;        // bytes of payload after the header
//!     u8       interface_type;      // IANA-style ifType (1..=259, see pymobiledevice3 INTERFACE_NAMES)
//!     u16 BE  unit;                 // interface unit (e.g. en0 → 0)
//!     u8       io;                  // 0 = in, 1 = out
//!     u32 BE  protocol_family;      // BSD AF_*: 2=AF_INET, 30=AF_INET6
//!     u32 BE  frame_pre_length;     // bytes of link-layer header at start of payload
//!     u32 BE  frame_post_length;
//!     char     interface_name[16];  // null-padded UTF-8 (e.g. "en0\0…", "pdp_ip0\0…")
//!     u32 LE  pid;                  // <-- LE, not BE
//!     char     comm[17];            // null-padded process name
//!     u32 BE  svc;                  // socket service class (libnetcore SO_NET_SERVICE_TYPE)
//!     u32 LE  epid;                 // effective pid (or 0)
//!     char     ecomm[17];           // effective process name
//!     u32 BE  seconds;              // capture time (Unix seconds)
//!     u32 BE  microseconds;
//!     /* …header_length bytes total… */
//!     u8       data[packet_length];
//! }
//! ```
//!
//! The host then writes a standard **libpcap** capture file (`tcpdump`-compatible / Wireshark) with
//! `LINKTYPE_ETHERNET = 1`. When `frame_pre_length == 0` (typical for `pdp_ip*` cellular) the device
//! payload is a bare IPv4/IPv6 datagram, so we prepend a synthetic Ethernet II header with EtherType
//! deduced from `protocol_family` to keep Wireshark dissectors happy. This matches the trick
//! pymobiledevice3 uses (with a hardcoded `0x0800` IPv4 frame; we do `0x86dd` for `AF_INET6` so v6
//! traffic decodes too).

use plist::Value;

/// Lockdown service name (current iOS / pymobiledevice3).
pub const PCAPD_SERVICE: &str = "com.apple.pcapd";

/// Reject unreasonable u32 BE plist frames. iOS RVI frames cap well below this.
pub const MAX_PCAPD_FRAME: usize = 16 * 1024 * 1024;

/// libpcap global header magic — microsecond resolution, **little-endian** layout (the variant
/// Wireshark / `tcpdump -r` accept on every platform).
pub const PCAP_MAGIC_USEC_LE: u32 = 0xa1b2_c3d4;

/// libpcap data link type — Ethernet II frames (with the synthetic header we prepend for raw IP
/// payloads). Same value pymobiledevice3 picks (`LINKTYPE_ETHERNET`).
pub const LINKTYPE_ETHERNET: u32 = 1;

/// Snapshot length we advertise in the pcap header. iOS device packets are MTU-sized.
pub const PCAP_DEFAULT_SNAPLEN: u32 = 65_535;

/// IANA `ifType` values we surface as friendly strings (subset of pymobiledevice3 `INTERFACE_NAMES`,
/// covering everything iOS actually emits on RVI).
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_ETHERNET: u8 = 6; // ethernetCsmacd
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_PPP: u8 = 23;
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_LOOPBACK: u8 = 24; // softwareLoopback
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_SLIP: u8 = 28;
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_CELLULAR: u8 = 243; // wwanPP (cellular pdp_ip*)
pub const PCAPD_IFACE_WWAN_PP2: u8 = 244;

/// EtherType for IPv4 / IPv6 — used in the synthetic Ethernet header for `pdp_ip*` packets.
pub const ETHERTYPE_IPV4: u16 = 0x0800;
pub const ETHERTYPE_IPV6: u16 = 0x86dd;

/// BSD `AF_*` values that travel inside the device header.
pub const AF_UNSPEC: u32 = 0;
pub const AF_INET: u32 = 2;
pub const AF_INET6: u32 = 30;

/// Synthetic source/destination MAC for cellular packets (matches pymobiledevice3 `\xbe\xef`*6).
pub const FAKE_MAC: [u8; 6] = [0xbe, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xef];

/// Decoded **`device_packet_struct`** payload. All cstring fields are already trimmed at the first
/// `\0` and decoded with lossy UTF-8 (control bytes never make it into the cstrings on iOS).
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PcapdRecord {
    pub header_length: u32,
    pub header_version: u8,
    pub packet_length: u32,
    pub interface_type: u8,
    pub unit: u16,
    /// `0 = inbound`, `1 = outbound` (matches pymobiledevice3).
    pub io: u8,
    pub protocol_family: u32,
    pub frame_pre_length: u32,
    pub frame_post_length: u32,
    pub interface_name: String,
    pub pid: u32,
    pub comm: String,
    pub svc: u32,
    pub epid: u32,
    pub ecomm: String,
    /// Unix seconds for the packet (device clock). `0` is rare but possible on early boot — fall
    /// back to host time at write site if you care about Wireshark's "Time of day".
    pub seconds: u32,
    pub microseconds: u32,
    /// Raw payload exactly as emitted by the device — `frame_pre_length` link-layer prefix plus
    /// IP / TCP / etc. Use [`build_pcap_record_payload`] to get a Wireshark-friendly Ethernet
    /// frame.
    pub data: Vec<u8>,
}

/// Length of the libpcap **global** header (RFC 1761). Matches `tcpdump`'s `pcap_file_header`.
pub const PCAP_GLOBAL_HEADER_LEN: usize = 24;
/// Length of each libpcap **record** header.
pub const PCAP_RECORD_HEADER_LEN: usize = 16;

/// Build the 24-byte libpcap global header (microsecond resolution, native LE magic).
pub fn build_pcap_global_header(snaplen: u32, link_type: u32) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut h = Vec::with_capacity(PCAP_GLOBAL_HEADER_LEN);
    h.extend_from_slice(&PCAP_MAGIC_USEC_LE.to_le_bytes()); // magic
    h.extend_from_slice(&2u16.to_le_bytes()); // version_major
    h.extend_from_slice(&4u16.to_le_bytes()); // version_minor
    h.extend_from_slice(&0i32.to_le_bytes()); // thiszone (UTC)
    h.extend_from_slice(&0u32.to_le_bytes()); // sigfigs
    h.extend_from_slice(&snaplen.to_le_bytes());
    h.extend_from_slice(&link_type.to_le_bytes());
    h
}

/// Build the 16-byte libpcap **record** header. Caller writes `payload_bytes` immediately after.
pub fn build_pcap_record_header(seconds: u32, microseconds: u32, payload_len: u32) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut h = Vec::with_capacity(PCAP_RECORD_HEADER_LEN);
    h.extend_from_slice(&seconds.to_le_bytes());
    h.extend_from_slice(&microseconds.to_le_bytes());
    h.extend_from_slice(&payload_len.to_le_bytes()); // incl_len
    h.extend_from_slice(&payload_len.to_le_bytes()); // orig_len
    h
}

/// Pick the EtherType byte pair to staple onto a raw IP packet, based on the device's
/// `protocol_family`. Falls back to IPv4 for unknown families (matches pymobiledevice3 default
/// `\x08\x00`).
pub fn ethertype_for_protocol_family(pf: u32) -> u16 {
    match pf {
        AF_INET6 => ETHERTYPE_IPV6,
        _ => ETHERTYPE_IPV4,
    }
}

/// Convert a [`PcapdRecord`] into the **frame bytes** we should write to the libpcap file:
///
/// - When the device included its own link-layer header (`frame_pre_length > 0`) we emit
///   `data` verbatim — Wireshark dissects it as Ethernet/PPP based on the link type.
/// - When `frame_pre_length == 0` (cellular `pdp_ip*` typically) we prepend a synthetic Ethernet
///   II header (`be:ef:be:ef:be:ef` → `be:ef:be:ef:be:ef`, EtherType from `protocol_family`).
/// - Special case: `interface_name == "pdp_ip"` *with* a non-zero `frame_pre_length` — pymobiledevice3
///   strips the first 4 bytes (a BSD-style `AF_INET` family header) and prepends the synthetic
///   Ethernet header. We do the same so the resulting capture stays Ethernet-typed.
pub fn build_pcap_record_payload(rec: &PcapdRecord) -> Vec<u8> {
    let ethertype = ethertype_for_protocol_family(rec.protocol_family);
    let needs_eth = rec.frame_pre_length == 0;
    let strip_pdp_prefix = rec.frame_pre_length > 0 && rec.interface_name == "pdp_ip";
    if needs_eth || strip_pdp_prefix {
        let body = if strip_pdp_prefix && rec.data.len() >= 4 {
            &rec.data[4..]
        } else {
            &rec.data[..]
        };
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(14 + body.len());
        out.extend_from_slice(&FAKE_MAC); // dst
        out.extend_from_slice(&FAKE_MAC); // src
        out.extend_from_slice(&ethertype.to_be_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(body);
        out
    } else {
        rec.data.clone()
    }
}

fn cstring_from_padded(bytes: &[u8]) -> String {
    let end = bytes.iter().position(|&b| b == 0).unwrap_or(bytes.len());
    String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes[..end]).into_owned()
}

fn read_u32_be(buf: &[u8], off: usize, what: &str) -> Result<u32, String> {
    let slice = buf
        .get(off..off + 4)
        .ok_or_else(|| format!("pcapd: short header reading {} at offset {}", what, off))?;
    Ok(u32::from_be_bytes(slice.try_into().unwrap()))
}

fn read_u32_le(buf: &[u8], off: usize, what: &str) -> Result<u32, String> {
    let slice = buf
        .get(off..off + 4)
        .ok_or_else(|| format!("pcapd: short header reading {} at offset {}", what, off))?;
    Ok(u32::from_le_bytes(slice.try_into().unwrap()))
}

fn read_u16_be(buf: &[u8], off: usize, what: &str) -> Result<u16, String> {
    let slice = buf
        .get(off..off + 2)
        .ok_or_else(|| format!("pcapd: short header reading {} at offset {}", what, off))?;
    Ok(u16::from_be_bytes(slice.try_into().unwrap()))
}

fn read_u8(buf: &[u8], off: usize, what: &str) -> Result<u8, String> {
    buf.get(off)
        .copied()
        .ok_or_else(|| format!("pcapd: short header reading {} at offset {}", what, off))
}

fn read_padded<'a>(buf: &'a [u8], off: usize, len: usize, what: &str) -> Result<&'a [u8], String> {
    buf.get(off..off + len)
        .ok_or_else(|| format!("pcapd: short header reading {} at offset {}", what, off))
}

/// Parse the **packed device header** (no surrounding plist) into a [`PcapdRecord`]. Public so
/// callers in tests / other tools can supply pre-extracted bytes.
pub fn parse_pcapd_record_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<PcapdRecord, String> {
    let header_length = read_u32_be(bytes, 0, "header_length")?;
    let header_version = read_u8(bytes, 4, "header_version")?;
    let packet_length = read_u32_be(bytes, 5, "packet_length")?;
    let interface_type = read_u8(bytes, 9, "interface_type")?;
    let unit = read_u16_be(bytes, 10, "unit")?;
    let io = read_u8(bytes, 12, "io")?;
    let protocol_family = read_u32_be(bytes, 13, "protocol_family")?;
    let frame_pre_length = read_u32_be(bytes, 17, "frame_pre_length")?;
    let frame_post_length = read_u32_be(bytes, 21, "frame_post_length")?;
    let iface_raw = read_padded(bytes, 25, 16, "interface_name")?;
    let pid = read_u32_le(bytes, 41, "pid")?;
    let comm_raw = read_padded(bytes, 45, 17, "comm")?;
    let svc = read_u32_be(bytes, 62, "svc")?;
    let epid = read_u32_le(bytes, 66, "epid")?;
    let ecomm_raw = read_padded(bytes, 70, 17, "ecomm")?;
    let seconds = read_u32_be(bytes, 87, "seconds")?;
    let microseconds = read_u32_be(bytes, 91, "microseconds")?;

    let header_len_us = header_length as usize;
    if header_len_us < 95 {
        return Err(format!(
            "pcapd: header_length {} smaller than fixed prefix (95)",
            header_length
        ));
    }
    let pkt_len = packet_length as usize;
    let total_needed = header_len_us
        .checked_add(pkt_len)
        .ok_or_else(|| "pcapd: header_length + packet_length overflow".to_string())?;
    if bytes.len() < total_needed {
        return Err(format!(
            "pcapd: payload short — need {} bytes, got {}",
            total_needed,
            bytes.len()
        ));
    }
    let data = bytes[header_len_us..total_needed].to_vec();

    Ok(PcapdRecord {
        header_length,
        header_version,
        packet_length,
        interface_type,
        unit,
        io,
        protocol_family,
        frame_pre_length,
        frame_post_length,
        interface_name: cstring_from_padded(iface_raw),
        pid,
        comm: cstring_from_padded(comm_raw),
        svc,
        epid,
        ecomm: cstring_from_padded(ecomm_raw),
        seconds,
        microseconds,
        data,
    })
}

/// Parse one **lockdown plist payload** (the `bplist00` blob inside a `u32 BE length + plist`
/// frame) into a [`PcapdRecord`]. The plist itself is a top-level CFData blob.
pub fn parse_pcapd_message(plist_bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<PcapdRecord, String> {
    let v: Value = plist::from_bytes(plist_bytes)
        .map_err(|e| format!("pcapd: plist decode failed: {}", e))?;
    let inner = match v {
        Value::Data(d) => d,
        Value::String(s) => s.into_bytes(),
        other => {
            return Err(format!(
                "pcapd: unexpected plist payload kind {:?} (expected CFData)",
                kind_name(&other)
            ))
        }
    };
    parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&inner)
}

fn kind_name(v: &Value) -> &'static str {
    match v {
        Value::Array(_) => "Array",
        Value::Dictionary(_) => "Dictionary",
        Value::Boolean(_) => "Boolean",
        Value::Data(_) => "Data",
        Value::Date(_) => "Date",
        Value::Real(_) => "Real",
        Value::Integer(_) => "Integer",
        Value::String(_) => "String",
        Value::Uid(_) => "Uid",
        _ => "Unknown",
    }
}

/// Friendly interface-type label for human-readable status lines (`6 → ethernetCsmacd`,
/// `243 → wwanPP`, …). Unknown values become `"ifType=<n>"`.
pub fn interface_type_label(t: u8) -> String {
    match t {
        PCAPD_IFACE_ETHERNET => "ethernetCsmacd".into(),
        PCAPD_IFACE_PPP => "ppp".into(),
        PCAPD_IFACE_LOOPBACK => "softwareLoopback".into(),
        PCAPD_IFACE_SLIP => "slip".into(),
        PCAPD_IFACE_CELLULAR => "wwanPP".into(),
        PCAPD_IFACE_WWAN_PP2 => "wwanPP2".into(),
        71 => "ieee80211".into(),
        117 => "gigabitEthernet".into(),
        144 => "ieee1394".into(),
        n => format!("ifType={}", n),
    }
}

/// Human label for the BSD `protocol_family` field on the wire.
pub fn protocol_family_label(pf: u32) -> &'static str {
    match pf {
        AF_UNSPEC => "AF_UNSPEC",
        AF_INET => "AF_INET",
        AF_INET6 => "AF_INET6",
        _ => "AF_other",
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// Reproduce one packed `device_packet_struct` from the docstring layout. Useful as a
    /// fixture for the parser tests below — keeps every offset honest.
    fn build_packed_record(
        header_extra: usize,
        iface_name: &str,
        pid: u32,
        comm: &str,
        protocol_family: u32,
        frame_pre_length: u32,
        seconds: u32,
        microseconds: u32,
        data: &[u8],
    ) -> Vec<u8> {
        let header_length: u32 = (95 + header_extra) as u32;
        let mut buf = Vec::with_capacity(header_length as usize + data.len());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&header_length.to_be_bytes());
        buf.push(1); // header_version
        buf.extend_from_slice(&(data.len() as u32).to_be_bytes());
        buf.push(PCAPD_IFACE_CELLULAR); // interface_type — wwanPP
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes()); // unit
        buf.push(0); // io = inbound
        buf.extend_from_slice(&protocol_family.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&frame_pre_length.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes()); // frame_post_length

        let mut iface = [0u8; 16];
        let bytes = iface_name.as_bytes();
        iface[..bytes.len().min(16)].copy_from_slice(&bytes[..bytes.len().min(16)]);
        buf.extend_from_slice(&iface);

        buf.extend_from_slice(&pid.to_le_bytes());

        let mut comm_buf = [0u8; 17];
        let cb = comm.as_bytes();
        comm_buf[..cb.len().min(17)].copy_from_slice(&cb[..cb.len().min(17)]);
        buf.extend_from_slice(&comm_buf);

        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes()); // svc
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u32.to_le_bytes()); // epid

        let ecomm_buf = [0u8; 17];
        buf.extend_from_slice(&ecomm_buf);

        buf.extend_from_slice(&seconds.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&microseconds.to_be_bytes());

        // Optional extra header bytes — the device sometimes inserts vendor-specific fields after
        // the fixed prefix; we just zero them so `header_length > 95` paths get exercised.
        if header_extra > 0 {
            buf.extend(std::iter::repeat_n(0u8, header_extra));
        }
        buf.extend_from_slice(data);
        buf
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_minimal_pdp_ip_record_and_synthesises_ethernet_header() {
        // 8-byte IPv4 stub payload. `frame_pre_length=0` → we'll prepend a fake Ethernet header.
        let body = b"\x45\x00\x00\x08\x00\x00\x40\x00";
        let packed = build_packed_record(0, "pdp_ip0", 73, "trustd", AF_INET, 0, 0x6000_0000, 0, body);
        let rec = parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).expect("parse");
        assert_eq!(rec.interface_name, "pdp_ip0");
        assert_eq!(rec.pid, 73);
        assert_eq!(rec.comm, "trustd");
        assert_eq!(rec.protocol_family, AF_INET);
        assert_eq!(rec.frame_pre_length, 0);
        assert_eq!(rec.data, body);
        assert_eq!(rec.header_length, 95);

        let frame = build_pcap_record_payload(&rec);
        assert_eq!(frame.len(), 14 + body.len(), "Ethernet header prepended");
        assert_eq!(&frame[..6], &FAKE_MAC, "dst MAC");
        assert_eq!(&frame[6..12], &FAKE_MAC, "src MAC");
        assert_eq!(&frame[12..14], &ETHERTYPE_IPV4.to_be_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&frame[14..], body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn passes_through_packets_that_already_carry_a_link_layer_header() {
        // 14-byte Ethernet header + 4-byte body. `frame_pre_length` is non-zero and the iface
        // is **not** "pdp_ip", so we should emit the bytes verbatim.
        let mut full = Vec::new();
        full.extend_from_slice(&FAKE_MAC);
        full.extend_from_slice(&FAKE_MAC);
        full.extend_from_slice(&ETHERTYPE_IPV4.to_be_bytes());
        full.extend_from_slice(b"DEAD");
        let packed = build_packed_record(0, "en0", 1, "kernel", AF_INET, 14, 0, 0, &full);
        let rec = parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).unwrap();
        let frame = build_pcap_record_payload(&rec);
        assert_eq!(frame, full);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pdp_ip_with_frame_prefix_strips_first_4_bytes_then_prepends_eth() {
        // A `pdp_ip` packet *with* a 4-byte BSD AF header — pymobiledevice3 strips it.
        let mut bsd = vec![0u8, 0, 0, 2]; // 4-byte AF_INET prefix
        bsd.extend_from_slice(b"PAYLOAD!");
        let packed = build_packed_record(0, "pdp_ip", 4, "comd", AF_INET, 4, 0, 0, &bsd);
        let rec = parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).unwrap();
        let frame = build_pcap_record_payload(&rec);
        assert_eq!(frame.len(), 14 + 8, "stripped BSD prefix, kept body");
        assert_eq!(&frame[14..], b"PAYLOAD!");
    }

    #[test]
    fn ipv6_protocol_family_yields_ipv6_ethertype() {
        let body = b"v6stub";
        let packed = build_packed_record(0, "pdp_ip0", 1, "p", AF_INET6, 0, 0, 0, body);
        let rec = parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).unwrap();
        let frame = build_pcap_record_payload(&rec);
        assert_eq!(&frame[12..14], &ETHERTYPE_IPV6.to_be_bytes());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_handles_extended_header_versions() {
        // Some firmwares set `header_length > 95` to reserve room for future fields. The parser
        // must skip those bytes and only return `data` from the offset the device declared.
        let body = b"abcd";
        let packed = build_packed_record(8, "en0", 9, "wifid", AF_INET, 14, 0, 0, body);
        let rec = parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(rec.header_length, 95 + 8);
        assert_eq!(rec.data, body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_rejects_truncated_payload_and_too_small_header() {
        // header_length < 95 must fail without panicking.
        let mut tiny = vec![0u8; 95];
        tiny[..4].copy_from_slice(&50u32.to_be_bytes());
        assert!(parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&tiny).is_err());

        // Promised packet_length larger than what we provided.
        let body = b"only-2";
        let mut packed = build_packed_record(0, "en0", 1, "x", AF_INET, 14, 0, 0, body);
        // Bump packet_length by 100 to make `total_needed` exceed buffer size.
        let lying = (body.len() as u32 + 100).to_be_bytes();
        packed[5..9].copy_from_slice(&lying);
        assert!(parse_pcapd_record_bytes(&packed).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_pcapd_message_unwraps_cfdata_blob() {
        let body = b"hello";
        let packed = build_packed_record(0, "en0", 1, "x", AF_INET, 14, 0, 0, body);
        let plist_bytes = {
            let mut buf = Vec::new();
            Value::Data(packed.clone())
                .to_writer_xml(&mut buf)
                .unwrap();
            buf
        };
        let rec = parse_pcapd_message(&plist_bytes).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(rec.data, body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_pcapd_message_rejects_non_data_root() {
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        Value::String("nope".into()).to_writer_xml(&mut buf).unwrap();
        // String is allowed (some firmwares emit binary as String); make sure we treat it as bytes
        // and then fail at the inner parse rather than the kind check.
        let err = parse_pcapd_message(&buf).unwrap_err();
        assert!(
            err.contains("short") || err.contains("header"),
            "unexpected error: {}",
            err
        );

        let mut dict_buf = Vec::new();
        Value::Dictionary(plist::Dictionary::new())
            .to_writer_xml(&mut dict_buf)
            .unwrap();
        let err = parse_pcapd_message(&dict_buf).unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.contains("Dictionary"), "unexpected error: {}", err);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pcap_global_header_layout_matches_libpcap_spec() {
        let h = build_pcap_global_header(PCAP_DEFAULT_SNAPLEN, LINKTYPE_ETHERNET);
        assert_eq!(h.len(), PCAP_GLOBAL_HEADER_LEN);
        assert_eq!(&h[..4], &PCAP_MAGIC_USEC_LE.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[4..6], &2u16.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[6..8], &4u16.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[8..12], &0i32.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[12..16], &0u32.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[16..20], &PCAP_DEFAULT_SNAPLEN.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[20..24], &LINKTYPE_ETHERNET.to_le_bytes());
    }

    #[test]
    fn pcap_record_header_uses_microsecond_resolution() {
        let h = build_pcap_record_header(0xdead_beef, 123_456, 999);
        assert_eq!(h.len(), PCAP_RECORD_HEADER_LEN);
        assert_eq!(&h[0..4], &0xdead_beef_u32.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[4..8], &123_456u32.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[8..12], &999u32.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(&h[12..16], &999u32.to_le_bytes());
    }

    #[test]
    fn ethertype_for_protocol_family_defaults_to_ipv4() {
        assert_eq!(ethertype_for_protocol_family(AF_INET), ETHERTYPE_IPV4);
        assert_eq!(ethertype_for_protocol_family(AF_INET6), ETHERTYPE_IPV6);
        assert_eq!(ethertype_for_protocol_family(0xff), ETHERTYPE_IPV4);
    }

    #[test]
    fn interface_type_label_recognises_known_apple_values() {
        assert_eq!(interface_type_label(PCAPD_IFACE_CELLULAR), "wwanPP");
        assert_eq!(interface_type_label(PCAPD_IFACE_ETHERNET), "ethernetCsmacd");
        assert_eq!(interface_type_label(PCAPD_IFACE_LOOPBACK), "softwareLoopback");
        assert_eq!(interface_type_label(0), "ifType=0");
    }

    #[test]
    fn protocol_family_label_covers_known_families() {
        assert_eq!(protocol_family_label(AF_UNSPEC), "AF_UNSPEC");
        assert_eq!(protocol_family_label(AF_INET), "AF_INET");
        assert_eq!(protocol_family_label(AF_INET6), "AF_INET6");
        assert_eq!(protocol_family_label(99), "AF_other");
    }
}

===== ARCHIVO: vendor/idevice-usb-core/src/screenshotr_proto.rs =====

//! `com.apple.mobile.screenshotr` protocol helpers — pure binary plist build/parse, no I/O.
//! Mirrors `libimobiledevice/src/screenshotr.c` + `device_link_service.c`.
//!
//! Wire format on the (TLS-wrapped) service socket: each message is **`u32` BE length + binary
//! plist (`bplist00`)**. Initial dialogue is a **DeviceLink** version exchange; the screenshot
//! request itself is a `DLMessageProcessMessage` envelope around a regular dictionary.
//!
//! The screenshotr service is only published when a **Developer Disk Image** is mounted on the
//! device. Lockdownd `StartService` returns the well-known error `"InvalidService"` otherwise.

use plist::Value;

/// Lockdown service name — same as **libimobiledevice** `SCREENSHOTR_SERVICE_NAME`.
pub const SCREENSHOTR_SERVICE: &str = "com.apple.mobile.screenshotr";

/// DeviceLink protocol version we advertise. `400.0` is what libimobiledevice screenshotr uses.
pub const SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR: u64 = 400;
pub const SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MINOR: u64 = 0;

/// Cap to refuse absurd `u32 BE` length-prefixed plist frames (PNG screenshots fit comfortably).
pub const MAX_SCREENSHOTR_FRAME: usize = 64 * 1024 * 1024;

fn to_binary_plist(value: &Value) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    let mut buf = Vec::new();
    value
        .to_writer_binary(&mut buf)
        .map_err(|e| format!("screenshotr: plist serialize: {}", e))?;
    Ok(buf)
}

/// Build the `DLMessageVersionExchange` reply we send to the device after we've received its
/// initial version array: `[ "DLMessageVersionExchange", "DLVersionsOk", <major:uint64> ]`.
pub fn build_dl_versions_ok(major: u64) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    let arr = Value::Array(vec![
        Value::String("DLMessageVersionExchange".into()),
        Value::String("DLVersionsOk".into()),
        Value::Integer(major.into()),
    ]);
    to_binary_plist(&arr)
}

/// `DLMessageProcessMessage` envelope: `[ "DLMessageProcessMessage", { "MessageType": "ScreenShotRequest" } ]`.
pub fn build_screenshot_request() -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    let mut inner = plist::Dictionary::new();
    inner.insert("MessageType".into(), Value::String("ScreenShotRequest".into()));
    let arr = Value::Array(vec![
        Value::String("DLMessageProcessMessage".into()),
        Value::Dictionary(inner),
    ]);
    to_binary_plist(&arr)
}

/// `[ "DLMessageDisconnect", "___EmptyParameterString___" ]` — best-effort goodbye, matches
/// `device_link_service_disconnect` in libimobiledevice.
pub fn build_disconnect() -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    let arr = Value::Array(vec![
        Value::String("DLMessageDisconnect".into()),
        Value::String("___EmptyParameterString___".into()),
    ]);
    to_binary_plist(&arr)
}

/// Length-prefix a serialized plist for the property_list_service wire format
/// (`u32 big-endian length + payload`). Used for both cleartext and TLS — we send the same
/// bytes, the secondary service TLS layer just encrypts them in place.
pub fn frame_plist_message(payload: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(4 + payload.len());
    out.extend_from_slice(&(payload.len() as u32).to_be_bytes());
    out.extend_from_slice(payload);
    out
}

fn parse_value(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Value, String> {
    plist::from_bytes(bytes).map_err(|e| format!("screenshotr: parse plist: {}", e))
}

fn dl_array(value: &Value) -> Result<&[Value], String> {
    let arr = value
        .as_array()
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: expected DL* plist array".to_string())?;
    if arr.is_empty() {
        return Err("screenshotr: DL* array is empty".into());
    }
    let cmd = arr[0]
        .as_string()
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: DL* command is not a string".to_string())?;
    if !cmd.starts_with("DL") {
        return Err(format!("screenshotr: not a DL* message: `{}`", cmd));
    }
    Ok(arr.as_slice())
}

/// Parse the initial `DLMessageVersionExchange` from the device, returning `(major, minor)`.
/// Mirrors the version check in `device_link_service_version_exchange`.
pub fn parse_dl_version_exchange(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(u64, u64), String> {
    let v = parse_value(bytes)?;
    let arr = dl_array(&v)?;
    let cmd = arr[0].as_string().unwrap_or("");
    if cmd != "DLMessageVersionExchange" {
        return Err(format!(
            "screenshotr: expected DLMessageVersionExchange, got `{}`",
            cmd
        ));
    }
    if arr.len() < 3 {
        return Err("screenshotr: DLMessageVersionExchange has unexpected format".into());
    }
    let major = arr[1]
        .as_unsigned_integer()
        .or_else(|| arr[1].as_signed_integer().map(|i| i as u64))
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: DLMessageVersionExchange major is not a number".to_string())?;
    let minor = arr[2]
        .as_unsigned_integer()
        .or_else(|| arr[2].as_signed_integer().map(|i| i as u64))
        .unwrap_or(0);
    Ok((major, minor))
}

/// `(major, minor)` from `parse_dl_version_exchange`: must be **≤** `SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_*`.
/// Returns a human-readable error so the CLI / WASM layer can surface it.
pub fn check_screenshotr_version(major: u64, minor: u64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if major > SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR
        || (major == SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR && minor > SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MINOR)
    {
        return Err(format!(
            "screenshotr: device DeviceLink version ({}.{}) > supported ({}.{})",
            major, minor, SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR, SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MINOR
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Verify a `DLMessageDeviceReady` reply (sent by the device after our `DLVersionsOk`).
pub fn parse_dl_device_ready(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), String> {
    let v = parse_value(bytes)?;
    let arr = dl_array(&v)?;
    let cmd = arr[0].as_string().unwrap_or("");
    if cmd != "DLMessageDeviceReady" {
        return Err(format!(
            "screenshotr: expected DLMessageDeviceReady, got `{}`",
            cmd
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Parse the screenshot `DLMessageProcessMessage` reply and return the raw image bytes
/// (`ScreenShotData`).
pub fn parse_screenshot_reply(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    let v = parse_value(bytes)?;
    let arr = dl_array(&v)?;
    let cmd = arr[0].as_string().unwrap_or("");
    if cmd != "DLMessageProcessMessage" {
        return Err(format!(
            "screenshotr: expected DLMessageProcessMessage, got `{}`",
            cmd
        ));
    }
    if arr.len() < 2 {
        return Err("screenshotr: DLMessageProcessMessage has no payload".into());
    }
    let dict = arr[1]
        .as_dictionary()
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: DLMessageProcessMessage payload is not a dict".to_string())?;
    let mt = dict
        .get("MessageType")
        .and_then(|v| v.as_string())
        .unwrap_or("");
    if mt != "ScreenShotReply" {
        if mt.is_empty() {
            return Err("screenshotr: ScreenShotReply missing MessageType".into());
        }
        return Err(format!(
            "screenshotr: expected ScreenShotReply, got `{}`",
            mt
        ));
    }
    let data = dict
        .get("ScreenShotData")
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: ScreenShotReply has no ScreenShotData".to_string())?
        .as_data()
        .ok_or_else(|| "screenshotr: ScreenShotData is not <data>".to_string())?;
    Ok(data.to_vec())
}

/// Image format detection used by `idevicescreenshot`: PNG on iOS 9+, TIFF on older releases.
pub fn detect_image_extension(bytes: &[u8]) -> &'static str {
    if bytes.len() >= 4 {
        if bytes.starts_with(b"\x89PNG") {
            return "png";
        }
        if &bytes[..4] == b"MM\x00\x2a" || &bytes[..4] == b"II\x2a\x00" {
            return "tiff";
        }
    }
    "dat"
}

/// MIME type matching the extension returned by [`detect_image_extension`].
pub fn detect_image_mime(bytes: &[u8]) -> &'static str {
    match detect_image_extension(bytes) {
        "png" => "image/png",
        "tiff" => "image/tiff",
        _ => "application/octet-stream",
    }
}

/// `screenshot-YYYY-MM-DD-HH-MM-SS` prefix (UTC), shape borrowed from `idevicescreenshot.c`.
/// Each caller passes its own broken-down time so this crate stays free of date-time deps.
pub fn default_screenshot_basename(
    year: i32,
    month: u8,
    day: u8,
    hour: u8,
    minute: u8,
    second: u8,
) -> String {
    format!(
        "screenshot-{:04}-{:02}-{:02}-{:02}-{:02}-{:02}",
        year, month, day, hour, minute, second
    )
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn round_trips_dl_versions_ok() {
        let bytes = build_dl_versions_ok(SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR).unwrap();
        let v: Value = plist::from_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();
        let arr = v.as_array().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(arr.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(arr[0].as_string().unwrap(), "DLMessageVersionExchange");
        assert_eq!(arr[1].as_string().unwrap(), "DLVersionsOk");
        assert_eq!(
            arr[2].as_unsigned_integer().unwrap(),
            SCREENSHOTR_VERSION_MAJOR
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_screenshot_reply_with_png() {
        let mut inner = plist::Dictionary::new();
        inner.insert("MessageType".into(), Value::String("ScreenShotReply".into()));
        inner.insert(
            "ScreenShotData".into(),
            Value::Data(b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\nfake-image-bytes".to_vec()),
        );
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![
            Value::String("DLMessageProcessMessage".into()),
            Value::Dictionary(inner),
        ]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        let img = parse_screenshot_reply(&buf).unwrap();
        assert!(img.starts_with(b"\x89PNG"));
        assert_eq!(detect_image_extension(&img), "png");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_mime(&img), "image/png");
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_higher_devicelink_version() {
        assert!(check_screenshotr_version(401, 0).is_err());
        assert!(check_screenshotr_version(400, 1).is_err());
        assert!(check_screenshotr_version(400, 0).is_ok());
        assert!(check_screenshotr_version(399, 99).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_version_exchange_array() {
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![
            Value::String("DLMessageVersionExchange".into()),
            Value::Integer(400u64.into()),
            Value::Integer(0u64.into()),
        ]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        let (maj, min) = parse_dl_version_exchange(&buf).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((maj, min), (400, 0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn detects_tiff_magic() {
        assert_eq!(detect_image_extension(b"MM\x00\x2axxxx"), "tiff");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_extension(b"II\x2a\x00xxxx"), "tiff");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_extension(b"binary-blob"), "dat");
    }

    #[test]
    fn detect_image_mime_matches_extension() {
        assert_eq!(detect_image_mime(b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n"), "image/png");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_mime(b"MM\x00\x2axxxx"), "image/tiff");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_mime(b"random"), "application/octet-stream");
        assert_eq!(detect_image_extension(b""), "dat");
    }

    #[test]
    fn build_screenshot_request_is_valid_dl_envelope() {
        let bytes = build_screenshot_request().unwrap();
        let v: Value = plist::from_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();
        let arr = v.as_array().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(arr.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(arr[0].as_string().unwrap(), "DLMessageProcessMessage");
        let inner = arr[1].as_dictionary().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            inner.get("MessageType").and_then(|v| v.as_string()),
            Some("ScreenShotRequest")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn build_disconnect_uses_canonical_empty_parameter_string() {
        let bytes = build_disconnect().unwrap();
        let v: Value = plist::from_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();
        let arr = v.as_array().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(arr[0].as_string().unwrap(), "DLMessageDisconnect");
        assert_eq!(arr[1].as_string().unwrap(), "___EmptyParameterString___");
    }

    #[test]
    fn frame_plist_message_prepends_u32_be_length() {
        let payload = b"hello bplist";
        let framed = frame_plist_message(payload);
        assert_eq!(framed.len(), 4 + payload.len());
        let len = u32::from_be_bytes(framed[0..4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
        assert_eq!(len, payload.len());
        assert_eq!(&framed[4..], payload);

        // Empty payload still gets a 4-byte length prefix.
        let empty = frame_plist_message(&[]);
        assert_eq!(empty, [0u8; 4]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_dl_device_ready_accepts_canonical_message() {
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![Value::String("DLMessageDeviceReady".into())]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        parse_dl_device_ready(&buf).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_dl_device_ready_rejects_non_ready_messages() {
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![Value::String("DLMessageDisconnect".into())]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        let err = parse_dl_device_ready(&buf).unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.contains("DLMessageDeviceReady"));

        // Non-DL command is also rejected.
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![Value::String("Something".into())]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        assert!(parse_dl_device_ready(&buf).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_screenshot_reply_rejects_wrong_message_type() {
        let mut inner = plist::Dictionary::new();
        inner.insert("MessageType".into(), Value::String("Bogus".into()));
        inner.insert("ScreenShotData".into(), Value::Data(vec![1, 2, 3]));
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![
            Value::String("DLMessageProcessMessage".into()),
            Value::Dictionary(inner),
        ]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        assert!(parse_screenshot_reply(&buf)
            .unwrap_err()
            .contains("ScreenShotReply"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_screenshot_reply_rejects_missing_data() {
        let mut inner = plist::Dictionary::new();
        inner.insert("MessageType".into(), Value::String("ScreenShotReply".into()));
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![
            Value::String("DLMessageProcessMessage".into()),
            Value::Dictionary(inner),
        ]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        assert!(parse_screenshot_reply(&buf)
            .unwrap_err()
            .contains("ScreenShotData"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_dl_version_exchange_rejects_wrong_command() {
        let arr = Value::Array(vec![
            Value::String("DLMessageBogus".into()),
            Value::Integer(400u64.into()),
            Value::Integer(0u64.into()),
        ]);
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        arr.to_writer_binary(&mut buf).unwrap();
        assert!(parse_dl_version_exchange(&buf).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn default_screenshot_basename_is_zero_padded() {
        assert_eq!(
            default_screenshot_basename(2026, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5),
            "screenshot-2026-01-02-03-04-05"
        );
    }
}

===== ARCHIVO: vendor/idevice-usb-core/src/tcp.rs =====

//! Pseudo-TCP over USB mux (blocking `BulkTransport`).

use crate::wire::{
    build_mux_setup_packet_v2, build_mux_version_request, parse_mux_version_response, MUX_MAGIC,
    MUX_PROTO_TCP, MUX_TCP_WINDOW,
};

pub const READ_BUF: usize = 65536;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Endpoints {
    pub ep_out: u8,
    pub ep_in: u8,
}

pub trait BulkTransport {
    fn bulk_out(&mut self, ep_num: u8, data: &[u8]) -> Result<(), String>;
    fn bulk_in(&mut self, ep_num: u8, max_len: usize) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>;
}

pub struct TcpConn {
    pub sport: u16,
    pub dport: u16,
    pub seq: u32,
    pub ack: u32,
    pub mux_version: u8,
    pub mux_rx_seq: u16,
    /// **Ciphertext stash for the rustls feeder**. iOS lockdownd can deliver a single mux segment
    /// that contains multiple back-to-back TLS records (~16 KiB each) — `pcapd` does this when
    /// the device is busy. rustls's internal plaintext buffer caps at `MAX_PLAINTEXT_SIZE`
    /// (16 KiB), so feeding the whole segment via `read_tls` in one go and *then* trying to drain
    /// fails with `received plaintext buffer full`. The TLS feeder pushes any unfed bytes here
    /// after each `read_tls` round-trip and consumes them on the next call, which keeps
    /// **feed → process → drain → repeat** cycle balanced.
    pub pending_tls_in: Vec<u8>,
}

impl TcpConn {
    #[inline]
    pub fn mux_header_len(&self) -> usize {
        if self.mux_version >= 2 {
            16
        } else {
            8
        }
    }

    /// State after VERSION (+ optional SETUP) so the next frame is SYN.
    pub fn new_ready_for_syn(mux_version: u8, dport: u16) -> Self {
        // Pseudo-TCP source port: avoid a single fixed value so reconnects (e.g. post-`Pair` → TLS)
        // do not collide on-device while an old mux session is still winding down.
        let mut b = [0u8; 2];
        let sport = if getrandom::getrandom(&mut b).is_ok() {
            0xc000u16 | (u16::from_le_bytes(b) & 0x3fff)
        } else {
            27015
        };
        Self {
            sport,
            dport,
            seq: 0,
            ack: 0,
            mux_version,
            mux_rx_seq: 0xFFFF,
            pending_tls_in: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// After SYN was sent, parse SYN-ACK `raw`, update seq/ack/mux_rx_seq, build final ACK frame.
    pub fn finish_handshake_ack_packet(
        &mut self,
        raw: &[u8],
        mux_tx_next: &mut u16,
    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
        let m = self.mux_header_len();
        if raw.len() < m + 20 {
            return Err("SYN-ACK too short".into());
        }
        if self.mux_version >= 2 && raw.len() >= 16 {
            self.mux_rx_seq = u16::from_be_bytes([raw[14], raw[15]]);
        }
        let tcp = m;
        let iph_seq = u32::from_be_bytes([raw[tcp + 4], raw[tcp + 5], raw[tcp + 6], raw[tcp + 7]]);
        let iph_ack =
            u32::from_be_bytes([raw[tcp + 8], raw[tcp + 9], raw[tcp + 10], raw[tcp + 11]]);
        self.seq = iph_ack;
        self.ack = iph_seq.wrapping_add(1);
        Ok(self.build_mux_tcp_packet(mux_tx_next, 0x10, &[]))
    }

    /// `mux_tx_next` must be shared across **all** pseudo-TCP streams on one USB mux session (mux v2);
    /// start at `1` for the first connection on a fresh USB link, then keep one counter for every further SYN/data on that link.
    pub fn handshake_and_connect(
        io: &mut impl BulkTransport,
        eps: &Endpoints,
        dport: u16,
        mux_tx_next: &mut u16,
    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
        let ver = build_mux_version_request();
        io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &ver)?;

        let resp = io.bulk_in(eps.ep_in, READ_BUF)?;
        let (major, _minor) = parse_mux_version_response(&resp)?;

        let mux_version = major;
        if mux_version >= 2 {
            let setup = build_mux_setup_packet_v2();
            io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &setup)?;
        }

        let mut conn = Self::new_ready_for_syn(mux_version, dport);
        let syn = conn.build_mux_tcp_packet(mux_tx_next, 0x02, &[]);
        io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &syn)?;
        let raw = io.bulk_in(eps.ep_in, READ_BUF)?;
        let ack_pkt = conn.finish_handshake_ack_packet(&raw, mux_tx_next)?;
        io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &ack_pkt)?;
        Ok(conn)
    }

    pub fn send(
        &mut self,
        io: &mut impl BulkTransport,
        eps: &Endpoints,
        mux_tx_next: &mut u16,
        payload: &[u8],
    ) -> Result<(), String> {
        let pkt = self.build_mux_tcp_packet(mux_tx_next, 0x10, payload);
        io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &pkt)?;
        self.seq = self.seq.wrapping_add(payload.len() as u32);
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn send_ack(
        &mut self,
        io: &mut impl BulkTransport,
        eps: &Endpoints,
        mux_tx_next: &mut u16,
    ) -> Result<(), String> {
        let pkt = self.build_mux_tcp_packet(mux_tx_next, 0x10, &[]);
        io.bulk_out(eps.ep_out, &pkt)
    }

    pub fn build_mux_tcp_packet(
        &mut self,
        mux_tx_next: &mut u16,
        flags: u8,
        payload: &[u8],
    ) -> Vec<u8> {
        let tcp_hdr_len = 20usize;
        let mux_len = self.mux_header_len();
        let total_len = (mux_len + tcp_hdr_len + payload.len()) as u32;

        let mut buf = Vec::with_capacity(total_len as usize);
        buf.extend_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_TCP.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&total_len.to_be_bytes());
        if self.mux_version >= 2 {
            buf.extend_from_slice(&MUX_MAGIC.to_be_bytes());
            buf.extend_from_slice(&mux_tx_next.to_be_bytes());
            buf.extend_from_slice(&self.mux_rx_seq.to_be_bytes());
            *mux_tx_next = mux_tx_next.wrapping_add(1);
        }

        buf.extend_from_slice(&self.sport.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&self.dport.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&self.seq.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&self.ack.to_be_bytes());
        buf.push(0x50);
        buf.push(flags);
        let win = (MUX_TCP_WINDOW >> 8) as u16;
        buf.extend_from_slice(&win.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(payload);
        buf
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn mux_header_len_v1_vs_v2() {
        let mut c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(1, 62078);
        assert_eq!(c.mux_header_len(), 8);
        c.mux_version = 2;
        assert_eq!(c.mux_header_len(), 16);
    }

    #[test]
    fn new_ready_for_syn_initialises_state() {
        let c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(2, 62078);
        assert_eq!(c.dport, 62078);
        assert_eq!(c.seq, 0);
        assert_eq!(c.ack, 0);
        assert_eq!(c.mux_version, 2);
        // After SETUP the device expects rx_seq == 0xFFFF + 1 → 0; we seed 0xFFFF so the first
        // outgoing TX appears as `rx_seq=0xFFFF`, which matches usbmuxd's `device_version_input`.
        assert_eq!(c.mux_rx_seq, 0xFFFF);
        // Source port stays inside the upper-half range usbmuxd uses (0xC000–0xFFFF).
        assert!(c.sport >= 0xc000 || c.sport == 27015);
    }

    #[test]
    fn build_mux_tcp_packet_v2_layout_and_advances_tx_seq() {
        let mut c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(2, 62078);
        c.sport = 0xC123;
        c.seq = 0;
        c.ack = 0;
        let mut tx = 1u16;
        let pkt = c.build_mux_tcp_packet(&mut tx, 0x02 /* SYN */, &[]);
        assert_eq!(tx, 2, "tx seq must advance by 1");
        assert_eq!(pkt.len(), 16 + 20);

        // Mux header (16 bytes for v2)
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(pkt[0..4].try_into().unwrap()), MUX_PROTO_TCP);
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(pkt[4..8].try_into().unwrap()), 36);
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(pkt[8..12].try_into().unwrap()), MUX_MAGIC);
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([pkt[12], pkt[13]]), 1, "mux tx seq we just advanced");
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([pkt[14], pkt[15]]), 0xFFFF, "mux rx seq from new_ready_for_syn");

        // TCP header (RFC 793 layout, big-endian)
        let tcp = &pkt[16..];
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([tcp[0], tcp[1]]), 0xC123); // src
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([tcp[2], tcp[3]]), 62078); // dst
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes([tcp[4], tcp[5], tcp[6], tcp[7]]), 0); // seq
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes([tcp[8], tcp[9], tcp[10], tcp[11]]), 0); // ack
        // data offset (high nibble) * 4 = 20 bytes
        assert_eq!(tcp[12] >> 4, 5);
        // flags
        assert_eq!(tcp[13], 0x02);
        // window scaled like usbmuxd (`MUX_TCP_WINDOW >> 8`)
        assert_eq!(
            u16::from_be_bytes([tcp[14], tcp[15]]),
            (MUX_TCP_WINDOW >> 8) as u16
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn build_mux_tcp_packet_v1_omits_mux_magic_block() {
        let mut c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(1, 62078);
        let mut tx = 0u16;
        let pkt = c.build_mux_tcp_packet(&mut tx, 0x10, b"abc");
        // v1 mux header is 8 bytes, no magic / tx / rx fields.
        assert_eq!(pkt.len(), 8 + 20 + 3);
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(pkt[0..4].try_into().unwrap()), MUX_PROTO_TCP);
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(pkt[4..8].try_into().unwrap()), 31);
        // tx seq must NOT advance for v1.
        assert_eq!(tx, 0);
        // payload trailing
        assert_eq!(&pkt[pkt.len() - 3..], b"abc");
    }

    fn fabricate_syn_ack_v2(mux_rx_seq: u16, peer_seq: u32, peer_ack: u32) -> Vec<u8> {
        // 16 bytes mux header + 20 bytes TCP header (no payload).
        let mut p = Vec::with_capacity(36);
        p.extend_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_TCP.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&36u32.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&MUX_MAGIC.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&mux_rx_seq.to_be_bytes());

        // src/dst — finish_handshake_ack_packet doesn't validate them but the offsets matter.
        p.extend_from_slice(&62078u16.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&0xC123u16.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&peer_seq.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&peer_ack.to_be_bytes());
        p.push(0x50);
        p.push(0x12); // SYN-ACK
        p.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        p.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        p
    }

    #[test]
    fn finish_handshake_ack_packet_advances_seq_ack_and_rx_seq() {
        let mut c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(2, 62078);
        let raw = fabricate_syn_ack_v2(0x0001, 0xDEAD_BEEF, 0x0000_0001);
        let mut tx = 2u16;
        let ack_pkt = c.finish_handshake_ack_packet(&raw, &mut tx).unwrap();
        // After SYN-ACK we capture the device's mux_rx_seq.
        assert_eq!(c.mux_rx_seq, 0x0001);
        // seq ← peer ack, ack ← peer seq + 1 (SYN consumes one logical byte).
        assert_eq!(c.seq, 0x0000_0001);
        assert_eq!(c.ack, 0xDEAD_BEEF + 1);
        // build_mux_tcp_packet was called with flags=0x10 (ACK) so output is well-formed.
        assert_eq!(ack_pkt.len(), 16 + 20);
        assert_eq!(ack_pkt[16 + 13], 0x10);
        // tx must have been bumped exactly once (the ACK frame).
        assert_eq!(tx, 3);
    }

    #[test]
    fn finish_handshake_ack_packet_rejects_truncated_input() {
        let mut c = TcpConn::new_ready_for_syn(2, 62078);
        let mut tx = 0u16;
        assert!(c.finish_handshake_ack_packet(&[0u8; 4], &mut tx).is_err());
    }
}

===== ARCHIVO: vendor/idevice-usb-core/src/wire.rs =====

//! Big-endian mux constants and static packets (matches usbmuxd `device.c`).

pub const APPLE_VENDOR_CLASS: u8 = 255;
pub const APPLE_VENDOR_SUBCLASS: u8 = 254;

pub const MUX_PROTO_VERSION: u32 = 0;
/// Device → host control / status (see `usbmuxd` `MUX_PROTO_CONTROL` / `device_control_input`).
pub const MUX_PROTO_CONTROL: u32 = 1;
pub const MUX_PROTO_SETUP: u32 = 2;
pub const MUX_PROTO_TCP: u32 = 6;

pub const MUX_MAGIC: u32 = 0xfeedface;

/// usbmuxd uses `tx_win = 131072` and `th_win = htons(tx_win >> 8)` in `send_tcp`.
pub const MUX_TCP_WINDOW: u32 = 131_072;

/// Appended to WebUSB `selectConfiguration` errors on Linux (browser + native CLI).
pub const SELECT_CONFIGURATION_FAILED_LINUX_HINT: &str = "\n\n\
On Linux, another stack often holds the device. In a terminal (then unplug/replug the cable and retry):\n\
  sudo systemctl stop usbmuxd\n\
  sudo systemctl stop usbmuxd.socket   # if usbmuxd is socket-activated and still respawns\n\
  sudo modprobe -r ipheth              # iPhone USB Ethernet; stop any network using that NIC first\n\
  systemctl --user stop gvfs-afc-volume-monitor.service";

/// Appended for **WebUSB** (browser) only: native tools can still work via usbmuxd while Chromium cannot.
pub const WEBUSB_CHROMIUM_USB_BUSY_EXPLAINER: &str = "\n\n\
A native stack may still reach the device over **usbmuxd** (`/var/run/usbmuxd`) while **Chromium WebUSB** cannot use that path — it must claim the USB interface directly. \
Either free the device for WebUSB using the hints above, or keep using a native client with usbmuxd.";

#[inline]
pub fn build_mux_version_request() -> [u8; 20] {
    let mut b = [0u8; 20];
    b[0..4].copy_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_VERSION.to_be_bytes());
    b[4..8].copy_from_slice(&20u32.to_be_bytes());
    b[8..12].copy_from_slice(&2u32.to_be_bytes());
    b[12..16].copy_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes());
    b[16..20].copy_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes());
    b
}

pub fn parse_mux_version_response(buf: &[u8]) -> Result<(u8, u8), String> {
    if buf.len() < 20 {
        return Err(format!("mux version reply too short: {} bytes", buf.len()));
    }
    let proto = u32::from_be_bytes(buf[0..4].try_into().map_err(|_| "proto")?);
    if proto != MUX_PROTO_VERSION {
        return Err(format!("expected mux VERSION (0), got protocol {}", proto));
    }
    let declared = u32::from_be_bytes(buf[4..8].try_into().map_err(|_| "len")?);
    if declared as usize > buf.len() {
        return Err(format!(
            "mux version length {} exceeds buffer {}",
            declared,
            buf.len()
        ));
    }
    let major = u32::from_be_bytes(buf[8..12].try_into().map_err(|_| "major")?);
    let minor = u32::from_be_bytes(buf[12..16].try_into().map_err(|_| "minor")?);
    if major != 1 && major != 2 {
        return Err(format!("unsupported mux version {}.{}", major, minor));
    }
    Ok((major as u8, minor as u8))
}

/// `MUX_PROTO_SETUP` with 1-byte payload `0x07` after mux v2 header (`device_version_input` in usbmuxd).
#[inline]
pub fn build_mux_setup_packet_v2() -> [u8; 17] {
    let mut b = [0u8; 17];
    b[0..4].copy_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_SETUP.to_be_bytes());
    b[4..8].copy_from_slice(&17u32.to_be_bytes());
    b[8..12].copy_from_slice(&MUX_MAGIC.to_be_bytes());
    b[12..14].copy_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
    b[14..16].copy_from_slice(&0xFFFFu16.to_be_bytes());
    b[16] = 0x07;
    b
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn version_request_matches_usbmuxd_layout() {
        let v = build_mux_version_request();
        assert_eq!(v.len(), 20);
        // protocol = MUX_PROTO_VERSION (0)
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(v[0..4].try_into().unwrap()), 0);
        // declared length = 20
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(v[4..8].try_into().unwrap()), 20);
        // major = 2
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(v[8..12].try_into().unwrap()), 2);
        // minor = 0
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(v[12..16].try_into().unwrap()), 0);
        // tail = 0
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(v[16..20].try_into().unwrap()), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_version_response_accepts_v1_and_v2() {
        // Build a fake VERSION reply: protocol=0 length=20 major=2 minor=0 …
        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 20];
        buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_VERSION.to_be_bytes());
        buf[4..8].copy_from_slice(&20u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf[8..12].copy_from_slice(&2u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf[12..16].copy_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes());
        let (maj, min) = parse_mux_version_response(&buf).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((maj, min), (2, 0));

        buf[8..12].copy_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes());
        let (maj, _) = parse_mux_version_response(&buf).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(maj, 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_version_response_rejects_short_or_wrong_proto() {
        assert!(parse_mux_version_response(&[0u8; 4]).is_err());

        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 20];
        buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&7u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf[4..8].copy_from_slice(&20u32.to_be_bytes());
        assert!(parse_mux_version_response(&buf).is_err());

        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 20];
        buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_VERSION.to_be_bytes());
        buf[4..8].copy_from_slice(&20u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf[8..12].copy_from_slice(&7u32.to_be_bytes()); // unsupported major
        assert!(parse_mux_version_response(&buf).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_version_response_rejects_bad_declared_length() {
        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 20];
        buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&MUX_PROTO_VERSION.to_be_bytes());
        // declared > buffer
        buf[4..8].copy_from_slice(&999u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf[8..12].copy_from_slice(&2u32.to_be_bytes());
        assert!(parse_mux_version_response(&buf).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn setup_packet_v2_has_magic_and_payload_byte() {
        let s = build_mux_setup_packet_v2();
        assert_eq!(s.len(), 17);
        // protocol = MUX_PROTO_SETUP (2)
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(s[0..4].try_into().unwrap()), MUX_PROTO_SETUP);
        // length = 17
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(s[4..8].try_into().unwrap()), 17);
        // mux magic
        assert_eq!(u32::from_be_bytes(s[8..12].try_into().unwrap()), MUX_MAGIC);
        // tx seq = 0, rx seq = 0xFFFF (so first device frame can have tx seq 1)
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([s[12], s[13]]), 0);
        assert_eq!(u16::from_be_bytes([s[14], s[15]]), 0xFFFF);
        // payload byte = 0x07 (SETUP "v2 capable" indicator)
        assert_eq!(s[16], 0x07);
    }

    #[test]
    fn proto_constants_match_usbmuxd_values() {
        assert_eq!(MUX_PROTO_VERSION, 0);
        assert_eq!(MUX_PROTO_CONTROL, 1);
        assert_eq!(MUX_PROTO_SETUP, 2);
        assert_eq!(MUX_PROTO_TCP, 6);
        assert_eq!(MUX_MAGIC, 0xfeedface);
        assert_eq!(MUX_TCP_WINDOW, 131_072);
    }
}
